KASH5

UniProt ID: Q8N6L0
Organism: Homo sapiens
Review Status: COMPLETE
📝 Provide Detailed Feedback
178 Annotations 1 Research 2 Core Functions 19 References 1 Documents 3 Questions 2 Experiments Raw YAML

Gene Description

KASH5 (formerly CCDC155) is a meiosis-specific, tail-anchored KASH protein of the outer nuclear membrane, expressed in spermatocytes and oocytes. Its short C-terminal KASH peptide (residues ~542-562, divergent from nesprins and carrying a 545-PPP-547 motif) crosses into the perinuclear space and binds SUN domain trimers of SUN1 (and SUN2), forming the meiotic LINC complex as SUN-KASH 6:6 assemblies at sites where telomeres attach to the nuclear envelope from leptotene to diplotene. Its large cytoplasmic region comprises an N-terminal EF-hand pair and a long coiled coil that mediates homodimerization; the EF-hands bind the dynein light intermediate chain, making KASH5 a transmembrane dynein activating adaptor that assembles dynein-dynactin (with LIS1) into processive complexes. By coupling telomeres through SUN1/SUN2 to cytoplasmic dynein and microtubules, KASH5 drives the rapid telomere-led chromosome movements and telomere clustering of meiotic prophase I that promote homologue pairing and synapsis. Kash5-null mice of both sexes are infertile with prophase I arrest, and biallelic human KASH5 variants cause non-obstructive azoospermia (SPGF88) and premature ovarian insufficiency (POF22). In mouse oocytes KASH5 also relocates to the meiotic spindle poles and has been implicated in meiotic resumption and spindle formation.

Existing Annotations Review

GO Term Evidence Action Reason
GO:0000781 chromosome, telomeric region
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		MARK AS OVER ANNOTATED
Summary: KASH5 is concentrated at nuclear-envelope sites where meiotic telomeres attach, from leptotene to diplotene.
Reason: KASH5 co-localizes with telomeres at light-microscopy resolution, but it is a tail-anchored outer nuclear membrane protein whose bulk faces the cytoplasm and whose KASH peptide reaches only the perinuclear space; it contacts telomeres indirectly via SUN1/SUN2 across the inner nuclear membrane. It is therefore not a component of the chromosomal telomeric region. Its location is better captured by nuclear outer membrane (GO:0005640) and the meiotic LINC complex term (GO:0034993).
Propagation Review
Root cause: TERM SCOPING PROBLEM
Failure modes: COMPARTMENT OR COMPLEX MISMATCH
Sources checked:
PANTHER:PTN002933398 SUPPORTS SOURCE BUT NOT TARGET
KASH5 clade node; human KASH5 is correctly inside the clade (true ortholog), so the issue is the scope of the descendant assertion, not node placement.
MGI:MGI:2687329 · Kash5 SOURCE WEAK OR INFERRED
Mouse Kash5 co-localization with telomeres at light-microscopy resolution; the protein is in the outer nuclear membrane, not the chromosome.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:22826121
These results indicate that KASH5 is a telomere-associated protein at early meiosis during both male and female meiosis.
PMID:22826121
KASH5 associates with the dynein–dynactin complexes, whereas SUN1 may associate with telomeres on the opposite outsides of the NE, thus transferring the driving force generated by cytoplasmic microtubules to telomeres in the nucleus.

Single-pass type IV
GO:0000800 lateral element
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		MARK AS OVER ANNOTATED
Summary: Lateral element (synaptonemal complex axis) localization transferred from mouse Kash5.
Reason: KASH5 foci sit at the telomeric ends of SCP3-stained chromosome axes because telomeres attach to the nuclear envelope, but KASH5 is an outer nuclear membrane protein, separated from the chromosome axis by the nuclear envelope. It is not a synaptonemal complex / lateral element component; this CC is an over-interpretation of co-localization at axis ends.

Single-pass type IV
GO:0005515 protein binding
IPI
PMID:25416956
A proteome-scale map of the human interactome network. 		REMOVE
Summary: High-throughput binary interaction (Rolland et al. 2014 proteome-scale yeast two-hybrid map (HI-II-14)) with UniProtKB:A8K660.
Reason: GO:0005515 protein binding is uninformative. Removal does not imply the reported interaction is false, but this single systematic screen hit does not indicate what KASH5 does. Most KASH5 high-throughput Y2H partners are unrelated transmembrane or ER/Golgi proteins (SNAREs, SLC transporters, TMEMs), a pattern typical of the hydrophobic C-terminal tail-anchor of KASH5 rather than a biological role; none has been connected to KASH5 function in meiocytes. The biologically established partners (SUN1/SUN2, dynein light intermediate chain, dynactin, LIS1) are captured by other terms.

Reason: GO:0005515 protein binding is uninformative. Removal does not imply the reported interaction is false, but this single systematic screen hit does not indicate what KASH5 does. Most KASH5 high-throughput Y2H partners are unrelated transmembrane or ER/Golgi proteins (SNAREs, SLC transporters, TMEMs), a pattern typical of the hydrophobic C-terminal tail-anchor of KASH5 rather than a biological role; none has been connected to KASH5 function in meiocytes. The biologically established partners (SUN1/SUN2, dynein light intermediate chain, dynactin, LIS1) are captured by other terms.
GO:0005515 protein binding

A reference map of the human binary protein interactome. 		REMOVE
Summary: High-throughput binary interaction (Luck et al. 2020 HuRI reference binary interactome (yeast two-hybrid)) with UniProtKB:P52803.
Reason: GO:0005515 protein binding is uninformative. Removal does not imply the reported interaction is false, but this single systematic screen hit does not indicate what KASH5 does. Most KASH5 high-throughput Y2H partners are unrelated transmembrane or ER/Golgi proteins (SNAREs, SLC transporters, TMEMs), a pattern typical of the hydrophobic C-terminal tail-anchor of KASH5 rather than a biological role; none has been connected to KASH5 function in meiocytes. The biologically established partners (SUN1/SUN2, dynein light intermediate chain, dynactin, LIS1) are captured by other terms.
GO:0005515 protein binding

A reference map of the human binary protein interactome. 		REMOVE
Summary: High-throughput binary interaction (Luck et al. 2020 HuRI reference binary interactome (yeast two-hybrid)) with UniProtKB:Q9H490.
Reason: GO:0005515 protein binding is uninformative. Removal does not imply the reported interaction is false, but this single systematic screen hit does not indicate what KASH5 does. Most KASH5 high-throughput Y2H partners are unrelated transmembrane or ER/Golgi proteins (SNAREs, SLC transporters, TMEMs), a pattern typical of the hydrophobic C-terminal tail-anchor of KASH5 rather than a biological role; none has been connected to KASH5 function in meiocytes. The biologically established partners (SUN1/SUN2, dynein light intermediate chain, dynactin, LIS1) are captured by other terms.

GO:0005515 protein binding
IPI
PMID:33058875
Structural Analysis of Different LINC Complexes Reveals Dist... 		MODIFY
Summary: Structural/biophysical evidence that the KASH5 C-terminal KASH peptide binds the SUN domain of SUN2, forming the meiotic LINC complex.
Reason: Generic protein binding is uninformative. The partner is a SUN-domain protein, and insertion of the KASH5 KASH peptide into the SUN domain trimer is how KASH5 is anchored in the outer nuclear membrane and how its dynein cargo is coupled through the nuclear envelope to telomeres; GO:0140444 cytoskeleton-nuclear membrane anchor activity captures this, consistent with the SUN1, SUN2, SYNE1 and SYNE2 reviews. (No KASH-domain/SUN-domain binding MF term exists in GO.)
Proposed replacements: cytoskeleton-nuclear membrane anchor activity
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:33058875
we solved high resolution crystal structures of human SUN2 in complex with the KASH-peptides of Nesprin3, Nesprin4, and KASH5
PMID:24062341
KASH5 is a dynein-binding protein tethered in the ONM via interactions with SUN1, an INM resident

GO:0007015 actin filament organization
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		MARK AS OVER ANNOTATED
Summary: Actin filament organization, from mouse oocyte knockdown data (reduced cytoplasmic F-actin mesh after Kash5 depletion).
Reason: KASH5 has no actin-binding domain; it is a dynein activating adaptor. The only support is a single siRNA/morpholino study in mouse oocytes reporting lower cytoplasmic F-actin intensity after SUN1 or KASH5 depletion, an indirect downstream consequence of disrupted maturation rather than KASH5 acting on actin filaments.
Propagation Review
Root cause: SOURCE WEAK OR INFERRED
Failure modes: SOURCE EVIDENCE WEAK ROLE CONFLATION
Sources checked:
PANTHER:PTN002933398 SOURCE WEAK OR INFERRED
KASH5 clade node; human KASH5 is correctly inside the clade (true ortholog), so the issue is the scope of the descendant assertion, not node placement.
MGI:MGI:2687329 · Kash5 SOURCE WEAK OR INFERRED
Mouse oocyte knockdown (PMID:26842404) with reduced F-actin mesh as an indirect downstream phenotype.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:26842404
As F-actin mesh level in cytoplasm was decreased in SUN1 or KASH5 depleted oocytes, the spindle migration would also be affected.
PMID:35703493
KASH5 is a bona fide activating adaptor that converts dynein and dynactin into a processive complex

GO:0007129 homologous chromosome pairing at meiosis
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		ACCEPT
Summary: Homologous chromosome pairing at meiosis.
Reason: Core process. Kash5-null spermatocytes arrest with homologues largely unpaired, and KASH5 does part of the work: it is the ONM dynein activating adaptor that supplies the motor force for telomere-led chromosome movements that promote pairing. Human KASH5 variants cause NOA and POI with defective pairing.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:24062341
In the absence of KASH5, spermatocytes arrest at the leptotene/zygotene stage of meiosis with the majority of chromosomes never forming homologous pairs.
PMID:24062341
KASH5 is therefore essential for the recruitment of dynein to telomere attachment sites at the NE during meiosis.
PMID:35703493
KASH5 is a bona fide activating adaptor that converts dynein and dynactin into a processive complex
GO:0034397 telomere localization
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		ACCEPT
Summary: Telomere localization (meiotic telomere movement/clustering at the nuclear envelope).
Reason: KASH5 recruits and activates dynein at telomere attachment sites, driving the telomere movements and bouquet-like clustering of meiotic prophase; in its absence SUN1/telomere foci never cluster. The more specific GO:0045141 meiotic telomere clustering is also appropriate and is used in core_functions.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:24062341
KASH5 is therefore essential for the recruitment of dynein to telomere attachment sites at the NE during meiosis.
PMID:24062341
Furthermore, we never observed clustered SUN1 foci in these cells.
PMID:22826121
KASH5 associates with the dynein–dynactin complexes, whereas SUN1 may associate with telomeres on the opposite outsides of the NE, thus transferring the driving force generated by cytoplasmic microtubules to telomeres in the nucleus.

GO:0034993 meiotic nuclear membrane microtubule tethering complex
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		ACCEPT
Summary: KASH5 is the outer-nuclear-membrane subunit of the meiotic LINC complex (SUN1:KASH5, and SUN2:KASH5).
Reason: Core complex. Here the meiotic term is exactly right: KASH5 is meiosis-specific, and crystal structures and SEC-MALS show SUN1-KASH5 6:6 assemblies (ComplexPortal CPX-2537; SUN2 variant CPX-7666).
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:33393904
SUN1-KASH4, SUN1-KASH5, and SUN1-KASH1 form 6:6 complexes in solution
PMID:24062341
KASH5 is a dynein-binding protein tethered in the ONM via interactions with SUN1, an INM resident
PMID:22826121
Collectively, these analyses indicate that KASH5 is localized to the NE near telomeres through the direct interaction with SUN1 in early meiosis.

GO:0042802 identical protein binding
IEA
GO_REF:0000107 		ACCEPT
Summary: Identical protein binding (self-association), transferred from mouse.
Reason: KASH5 self-associates through its central coiled coil and is a homodimer, the quaternary structure shared by dynein activating adaptors; this is part of its adaptor mechanism rather than an arbitrary interaction.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:24062341
Complementary coIP analyses confirmed that KASH5 self-association was mediated by the central coiled-coil domain
PMID:35703493
SEC-MALS analysis of NCC (alone) is consistent with a homodimer, defining a quaternary structure for KASH5 that is conserved among other dynein activating adaptors.
GO:0051225 spindle assembly
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		KEEP AS NON CORE
Summary: Spindle assembly in mouse oocytes (knockdown study).
Reason: Single mouse-oocyte knockdown study: KASH5 relocates to the spindle poles after germinal vesicle breakdown and its depletion gives small, abnormal spindles with loss of pericentrin and p150Glued from the poles. Plausible via KASH5-dependent dynein/dynactin localization, but secondary to its core meiotic prophase role and not yet confirmed genetically.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:26842404
However, after SUN1 or KASH5 depletion, PCNT was not detected at the spindle poles, which correlated with the aberrant spindle formation after SUN1 or KASH5 depletion
PMID:26842404
At MI, SUN1 and KASH5 appeared as dots around the forming spindle, and at MII stage, they both localized dominantly to the spindle poles.

IBA

GO:0070840 dynein complex binding
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		ACCEPT
Summary: Dynein complex binding.
Reason: Core MF. KASH5 binds cytoplasmic dynein directly via its N-terminal EF-hand pair engaging the dynein light intermediate chain helix 1, and co-purifies with dynein heavy/intermediate chains, dynactin p150 and LIS1. Beyond binding it activates processive dynein-dynactin motility (see NEW GO:0140660).
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:36946995
KASH5 interacts with a dynein light intermediate chain (DYNC1LI1 or DYNC1LI2) via a conserved helix in the LIC C-terminal
PMID:24062341
These data imply that KASH5 is an adaptor for cytoplasmic dynein.
PMID:22826121
Indeed, antibodies against KASH5 precipitated the dynactin p150 subunit as well as SUN1 from cytoplasmic extracts of mouse testis
PMID:35703493
KASH5 is a bona fide activating adaptor that converts dynein and dynactin into a processive complex

Suggested Questions for Experts

Q: Should the oocyte-derived IBA/IEA terms (actin filament organization, spindle localization, spindle assembly) remain on the KASH5 PAINT node, given they rest on a single mouse knockdown study with indirect phenotypes?

Suggested experts: PAINT curators, MGI curators

Q: How is the SUN1-KASH5 6:6 stoichiometry reconciled with the KASH5 homodimer that binds a single dynein LIC, and does SUN1 versus SUN2 pairing change force transmission at telomeres?

Suggested experts: LINC complex structural biologists, dynein biophysicists

Q: Is the KASH5 EF-hand pair calcium-independent in vivo, and is KASH5 dynein activation regulated during prophase I (e.g. by CDK or other kinases)?

Suggested experts: meiosis cell biologists

Suggested Experiments

Experiment: Knock-in mice carrying the KASH5 EF-hand/LIC-interface mutation that abolishes dynein binding in vitro, with live imaging of telomere movement and scoring of pairing/synapsis.

Hypothesis: Dynein activation by KASH5, rather than KASH5 presence at the NE per se, is required for telomere clustering and homologue pairing.

Type: knock-in genetics with live imaging

Experiment: Oocyte-specific conditional Kash5 deletion after prophase I (e.g. Gdf9- or Zp3-Cre) to test spindle assembly and meiotic resumption phenotypes reported by knockdown.

Hypothesis: KASH5 has a genuine post-prophase role at oocyte spindle poles.

Type: conditional knockout

KASH5/CCDC155 (human; UniProt Q8N6L0): functional-annotation report

Executive conclusion

KASH5 is not an enzyme or transporter. It is a meiosis-restricted, tail-anchored outer-nuclear-membrane protein that performs two linked functions: it is the cytoplasmic half of a meiotic LINC (linker of nucleoskeleton and cytoskeleton) complex, and it is a direct activating adaptor for cytoplasmic dynein-1. By binding SUN1—or, in some mouse contexts, SUN2—across the nuclear envelope while recruiting dynein–dynactin on the cytoplasmic side, KASH5 transmits microtubule-generated forces to chromosome telomeres. These forces drive telomere-led chromosome movements required for efficient homolog search, pairing, synapsis, recombination, and progression through meiotic prophase I (garner2023themeioticlinc pages 12-12, park2024cargospecificityregulation pages 5-5, kmonickova2020theroleof pages 6-9).

Human biallelic KASH5 variants are associated with non-obstructive azoospermia (NOA), meiotic arrest, diminished ovarian reserve or premature ovarian insufficiency, and recurrent miscarriage. A 2024 study extended the phenotype to recurrent androgenetic hydatidiform mole, although this last association rests on very limited case evidence (hou2023ahomozygouskash5 pages 1-2, rezaei2024defectsinmeiosis pages 1-2).

1. Mandatory identity verification

The requested target is correctly identified. Foundational literature explicitly equates CCDC155 with KASH5 in mammals and describes the human protein as an approximately 62.7-kDa KASH-domain protein. The reported architecture—an N-terminal EF-hand-like region, long central coiled coil, C-terminal transmembrane segment, and terminal KASH region—matches the supplied UniProt Q8N6L0/InterPro/Pfam annotation. No conflicting human gene with the same symbol was encountered (horn2013amammaliankash pages 2-3, morimoto2012aconservedkash pages 2-3).

The organism is Homo sapiens. Much of the organism-level mechanistic evidence nevertheless comes from mouse meiosis, whereas dynein activation has been established using purified proteins and human cultured-cell systems. The human disease evidence comes from rare families and functional characterization of individual variants. These evidence classes should not be conflated.

2. Protein architecture and membrane topology

KASH5 has an N-terminal cytoplasmic region containing tandem EF-hand-like domains, an extended coiled-coil region, and a C-terminal tail-anchor comprising a transmembrane segment followed by a short KASH sequence in the perinuclear space. Early comparative annotation placed the EF-hand-like region approximately at residues 18–109 and estimated a roughly 190-residue central coiled coil. The human and mouse proteins were estimated at approximately 62.7 and 64.4 kDa, respectively (horn2013amammaliankash pages 2-3).

The KASH terminus binds SUN-domain proteins in the lumen between the inner and outer nuclear membranes. Consequently, KASH5’s N-terminal EF-hand/coiled-coil region projects into the cytoplasm, where it engages dynein. In cultured human cells, KASH5 and its isolated KASH region localized to the nuclear envelope; dominant-negative SUN1 or combined SUN1/SUN2 depletion disrupted this localization, demonstrating SUN-dependent nuclear-envelope retention (horn2013amammaliankash pages 2-3).

The EF-hand pair is structurally and functionally important but should not be annotated simply as a conventional calcium sensor. Substitution of predicted calcium-coordinating positions impaired or abolished dynein binding, yet purified KASH5 did not detectably bind calcium and cellular calcium changes did not control the interaction. Current evidence therefore favors an EF-hand-derived structural interface for dynein LIC recognition rather than calcium-regulated signaling (garner2023themeioticlinc pages 13-14).

3. Cellular localization

KASH5 operates principally at the outer nuclear membrane of meiotic germ cells during prophase I. Its KASH tail contacts SUN1/SUN2 in the perinuclear space, while its large N-terminal region remains cytoplasmic. On the nucleoplasmic side, the SUN-containing assembly connects indirectly to telomeric proteins; on the cytoplasmic side, KASH5 recruits dynein, dynactin, and associated regulators (park2024cargospecificityregulation pages 5-5, kmonickova2020theroleof pages 6-9).

Mouse spermatocyte imaging showed KASH5 colocalization with the telomere protein TRF2 and SUN1 at chromosome ends. It appears from leptotene, persists through pachytene and diplotene, and disappears by metaphase I as telomeres detach from the nuclear envelope. Expression was detected mainly in testis and ovary during early oogenesis rather than broadly across somatic tissues, consistent with a meiosis-specialized protein (morimoto2012aconservedkash pages 2-3).

SUN1 is the principal partner, but SUN2 can form residual or alternative KASH5-containing meiotic LINC complexes in mouse spermatocytes. Thus, “SUN1–KASH5 complex” is the best primary annotation, while SUN2 provides partially redundant context-dependent support rather than an entirely separate pathway (kmonickova2020theroleof pages 6-9).

4. Primary molecular function: transmembrane dynein-activating adaptor

The decisive mechanistic advance was recognition that KASH5 does more than tether an already active motor: it directly activates cytoplasmic dynein-1. Purified-component and cell-recruitment studies showed that KASH5 engages either dynein light intermediate chain LIC1 or LIC2 and supports formation of motile dynein–dynactin complexes. It is therefore regarded as the first established transmembrane dynein-activating adaptor (garner2023themeioticlinc pages 12-12, park2024cargospecificityregulation pages 5-5, garner2023themeioticlinc pages 13-14).

The binding interface follows a mechanism shared with other dynein activating adaptors. A conserved helix in the C-terminal region of LIC1—helix 1, residues 440–456—binds the KASH5 EF-hand region. Deleting both LIC helices reduced KASH5 association to 8.0% of the full-length reference, while helix-1 mutations or deletion reduced pull-down to approximately 1.9–3.9% of wild type. A construct lacking only helix 2 retained 177% of reference pull-down, identifying helix 1 rather than helix 2 as the crucial element (garner2023themeioticlinc pages 11-12).

Complex assembly appears hierarchical. KASH5 first captures dynein through LIC; LIS1 promotes or stabilizes dynein’s open conformation; dynactin is then incorporated through dynein intermediate-chain/p150 interactions and broader contacts with the adaptor. Experimentally, disrupting dynactin did not prevent initial dynein/LIS1 recruitment to KASH5, whereas LIS1 depletion disproportionately impaired dynactin incorporation. A candidate Spindly-like motif around KASH5 residues 371–376 may contribute to dynactin engagement, but that specific interaction remains less firmly established (garner2023themeioticlinc pages 12-12, garner2023themeioticlinc pages 5-6).

KASH5 therefore has no catalytic substrate of its own. Its relevant “cargo” is the telomere-attached meiotic chromosome, coupled across the nuclear envelope by the SUN–KASH bridge. Dynein hydrolyzes ATP and walks along microtubules; KASH5 specifies, assembles, and activates that transport machinery at the nuclear envelope.

5. Biological process and pathway

The functional pathway can be summarized as:

meiotic telomere complex → SUN1/SUN2 at the inner nuclear membrane → KASH5 across the outer nuclear membrane → LIC-bound dynein + LIS1 + dynactin → microtubule-dependent telomere/chromosome movement.

During meiotic prophase I, telomeres attach to the nuclear envelope and undergo rapid movements, including transient clustering or “bouquet” organization. Restricting chromosome motion to the nuclear surface facilitates a two-dimensional homology search and helps align homologous chromosomes for synaptonemal-complex formation and crossover recombination. KASH5 is consequently upstream of normal homolog pairing and synapsis, although it is not itself a recombinase or structural component of the synaptonemal complex (park2024cargospecificityregulation pages 5-5, morimoto2012aconservedkash pages 2-3).

Mouse loss-of-function evidence shows that telomere attachment can persist in some contexts while effective homolog movement and pairing fail, followed by arrest before pachytene and infertility. This distinction indicates that KASH5’s central role is force coupling and motor activation, not merely passive attachment of telomeres to the envelope (hou2023ahomozygouskash5 pages 2-3).

6. Human genetics and reproductive phenotypes

Male infertility

Human biallelic variants consistently implicate KASH5 in severe spermatogenic failure, particularly meiotic arrest and NOA. The transmembrane variant p.Leu535Gln (L535Q) was reported homozygously in two azoospermic brothers. Functional studies showed that reduced transmembrane-segment hydrophobicity redirects L535Q KASH5 from the endoplasmic reticulum/outer nuclear membrane to mitochondria. The most plausible pathogenic mechanism is therefore loss of KASH5 from the meiotic nuclear envelope—not major mitochondrial toxicity—preventing assembly of the telomere–LINC–dynein apparatus (bentebbal2022ahumaninfertilityassociated pages 8-9, bentebbal2022ahumaninfertilityassociated pages 1-2).

In a 2023 consanguineous Pakistani family, homozygous c.1270_1273del, p.Arg424Thrfs*20 occurred in five siblings. The affected brother had absent testicular KASH5 protein, NOA, and meiotic arrest before pachytene. This human cellular phenotype closely matches the predicted consequence of failed telomere-driven chromosome movement and the mouse knockout phenotype (hou2023ahomozygouskash5 pages 1-2).

Female infertility and pregnancy loss

The same p.Arg424Thrfs*20 family showed marked sex-dependent expressivity. Four affected sisters had diminished ovarian reserve rather than complete ovarian failure. Three experienced at least three miscarriages each, all during the third month; another had never conceived but retained a dominant follicle at age 35. In cultured cells, the truncated protein still encircled the nucleus but interacted more weakly with SUN1. The authors proposed that residual function may permit folliculogenesis and fertilization while increasing production of chromosomally abnormal oocytes and subsequent miscarriage (hou2023ahomozygouskash5 pages 8-9, hou2023ahomozygouskash5 pages 1-2).

Other 2022 reports linked homozygous KASH5 variants to premature ovarian insufficiency through disordered homolog pairing and linked biallelic variants to male meiotic arrest/NOA. Collectively, the evidence supports an autosomal-recessive KASH5-related reproductive disorder with variable female severity, but the number of independently characterized families remains small (hou2023ahomozygouskash5 pages 11-11).

Hydatidiform mole: 2024 development

A November 2024 JCI study of meiosis-I genes identified a woman with three hydatidiform moles and two miscarriages carrying KASH5 p.Arg519 and p.Leu535Gln* variants. Their CADD scores were 36 and 25.6, respectively; ACMG interpretation remained cautious, even though L535Q already had functional and infertility evidence. This observation plausibly links defective maternal meiosis I to androgenetic mole formation, but it represents an emerging association rather than a population-level estimate or definitive expansion of the KASH5 phenotype (rezaei2024defectsinmeiosis pages 1-2, rezaei2024defectsinmeiosis pages 14-15).

7. Recent developments, 2023–2024

The major 2023 molecular development was detailed biochemical placement of KASH5 within the activating-adaptor family. Garner and colleagues resolved the dependence on LIC helix 1, showed that either LIC paralog can support recruitment, defined roles for LIS1 and dynactin, and demonstrated that the EF hands function structurally rather than as a conventional calcium switch (garner2023themeioticlinc pages 11-12, garner2023themeioticlinc pages 12-12, garner2023themeioticlinc pages 13-14).

The major 2023 clinical development was expansion from complete meiotic failure to a sex-dimorphic spectrum comprising male NOA, female diminished ovarian reserve, and recurrent miscarriage within one family. This finding argues against assuming that the same biallelic variant necessarily produces equivalent germ-cell depletion in both sexes (hou2023ahomozygouskash5 pages 8-9, hou2023ahomozygouskash5 pages 1-2).

In 2024, authoritative dynein reviews incorporated KASH5 as a newly established cargo-specific activating adaptor, confirming that the field now regards active motor assembly—not passive tethering—as its core molecular function. The recurrent-mole study further suggested that partial or variant-specific disruption may permit conception but predispose to abnormal meiotic products and pregnancy pathology (park2024cargospecificityregulation pages 5-5, rezaei2024defectsinmeiosis pages 1-2).

8. Current and potential applications

The immediate real-world application is genetic diagnosis and counseling, not targeted therapy. KASH5 is a rational gene-panel or exome-analysis candidate in:

  • NOA with spermatocyte arrest, especially zygotene/pre-pachytene arrest;
  • unexplained premature ovarian insufficiency or diminished ovarian reserve;
  • familial reproductive failure affecting both sexes;
  • recurrent miscarriage where a meiotic defect is suspected; and
  • possibly recurrent androgenetic hydatidiform mole.

Variant interpretation should incorporate domain location and functional assays. Transmembrane substitutions may alter organelle targeting, truncations may weaken SUN binding or remove the KASH tail, and N-terminal EF-hand/coiled-coil variants may impair dynein recruitment. Because reported families are few, pathogenic classification should not rely on gene plausibility alone; segregation, rarity, reproductive histology, protein localization, SUN interaction, and dynein-binding assays are particularly valuable (bentebbal2022ahumaninfertilityassociated pages 8-9, hou2023ahomozygouskash5 pages 1-2, rezaei2024defectsinmeiosis pages 1-2).

There is no established KASH5-specific drug, replacement strategy, or clinical trial. Assisted-reproduction decisions remain phenotype dependent. Complete male meiotic arrest predicts poor prospects for recovering mature sperm, whereas partially affected women may still ovulate but face diminished reserve, miscarriage, or abnormal conceptions. These implications require individualized reproductive-genetics counseling rather than a uniform prognosis.

9. Evidence quality and unresolved questions

The molecular annotation is strong because multiple independent approaches converge: meiotic colocalization, SUN-dependent envelope targeting, protein interaction mapping, purified-motor reconstitution, cell-based recruitment, mutagenesis, and mouse loss-of-function phenotypes. The conclusion that KASH5 is a dynein-activating LINC adaptor is considerably firmer than any individual disease association (garner2023themeioticlinc pages 11-12, garner2023themeioticlinc pages 12-12, horn2013amammaliankash pages 2-3, morimoto2012aconservedkash pages 2-3).

The human disease evidence is compelling but numerically limited. Most reports involve single consanguineous families or a few affected individuals; penetrance and genotype–phenotype relationships are therefore not yet reliably quantified. In particular, the determinants of female phenotypic variation—complete ovarian insufficiency versus diminished reserve, miscarriage, or molar pregnancy—remain unresolved.

Additional priorities include structural determination of a full KASH5–dynein–dynactin complex, clarification of SUN1 versus SUN2 usage in human meiocytes, definition of how KASH5 motor activity is temporally switched on and off, and larger genetic cohorts with detailed meiotic histology. The apparent calcium independence of its EF hands and the proposed dynactin-contact motif also merit direct high-resolution testing.

Final functional annotation

KASH5/CCDC155 (UniProt Q8N6L0) is a meiosis-specific, single-pass outer-nuclear-membrane KASH protein and dynein-1 activating adaptor. Its luminal C-terminal KASH tail binds SUN1/SUN2, while its cytoplasmic EF-hand-like and coiled-coil regions recruit dynein through LIC1/LIC2 and support LIS1/dynactin-dependent motor activation. This LINC–motor assembly transmits microtubule forces to telomeres during meiotic prophase I, enabling chromosome movement needed for homolog pairing, synapsis, recombination, and gametogenesis. Human biallelic loss-of-function or mistargeting variants cause a recessive reproductive phenotype encompassing meiotic-arrest NOA and variable female ovarian insufficiency, diminished reserve, miscarriage, and potentially recurrent androgenetic hydatidiform mole.

📚 Additional Documentation

Notes

(KASH5-notes.md)

KASH5 (CCDC155) review notes

2026-09-27 — initial review (claude-code)

Sources: UniProt Q8N6L0, GOA (177 rows; 147 are GO:0005515 protein binding), cached publications.
Falcon deep research not yet available at the time of review (see end of file for status).

Identity and architecture

  • Tail-anchored ONM protein, 562 aa; TM 522-542, perinuclear KASH peptide 543-562 [file:human/KASH5/KASH5-uniprot.txt "Single-pass type IV"].
  • N-terminal EF-hand pair (Pfam EF-hand_9), central coiled coil (164-349), C-terminal KASH peptide.
  • KASH peptide is divergent but acts as a canonical KASH domain PMID:22826121; carries a 545-PPP-547 motif used at the SUN1-KASH5 6:6 interface PMID:33393904.
  • Behaves as authentic KASH protein: NE targeting SUN1/2-dependent, KASH domain sufficient, displaces NESP2 PMID:24062341.

LINC partners

  • SUN1 (major; SUN1 KO abolishes KASH5 telomere localization) PMID:22826121.
  • SUN1-KASH5 crystal structure; 6:6 complexes PMID:33393904. SUN2-KASH5 structure PMID:33058875; SUN2-KASH5 less stable in solution than SUN1-KASH5 (PMID:33393904).
  • ComplexPortal CPX-2537 (SUN1-KASH5) and CPX-7666 (SUN2-KASH5).

Dynein activating adaptor

  • Recruits dynein IC and p150Glued to NE; co-IPs DHC, IC, p150, LIS1 PMID:24062341.
  • In vitro: converts dynein-dynactin into processive complex PMID:35703493; EF-hand pair binds LIC helix 1; homodimer.
  • Independent confirmation; LIC1/2 binding; LIS1 required for dynactin incorporation; dynein recruitment can be dynactin-independent PMID:36946995.
  • => Proposed NEW GO:0140660 cytoskeletal motor activator activity (IDA, PMID:35703493). Comparator note: GO:0140660 is barely used in human GOA (1 NAS); BICD2 carries dynein complex binding only. Kept NEW because the in vitro motility data directly meet the definition; GO:0070840 retained as ACCEPT.

Process

  • Kash5-/- mice: infertile both sexes, prophase I arrest, pairing fails although telomeres still attach to NE; no clustering of SUN1 foci; no dynein at telomere attachment sites PMID:24062341.
  • Human: L197P (coiled coil) blocks NE distribution and SUN1 NE enrichment; NOA + POI [PMID:35587281, abstract only].
  • Oocyte post-prophase: knockdown -> GV arrest, small spindles, low F-actin mesh; KASH5 at MII spindle poles PMID:26842404. Single knockdown study; used to judge spindle/actin IBA/IEA rows (non-core / over-annotated).

Decisions summary

  • Protein binding: 3 SUN1/SUN2 structural rows -> MODIFY to GO:0140444 (consistent with SUN1/SUN2/SYNE1/SYNE2 reviews); 144 HT Y2H rows -> REMOVE (uninformative; predominantly TM/ER-Golgi partners typical of tail-anchor Y2H artefacts).
  • Chromosome-side CCs (telomeric region, lateral element) -> MARK_AS_OVER_ANNOTATED: KASH5 is in the ONM, contacting telomeres only indirectly via SUN1/SUN2.
  • GO:0034993 is the right complex term for KASH5 (meiosis-specific).

Deep research (Falcon) — incorporated after arrival

  • Report arrived during review; consistent with the literature-based conclusions above: ONM tail-anchored KASH protein, SUN1 principal partner with SUN2 partially redundant, first transmembrane dynein activating adaptor, EF-hands bind LIC helix 1 without detectable Ca2+ binding [file:human/KASH5/KASH5-deep-research-falcon.md "It is therefore regarded as the first established transmembrane dynein-activating adaptor"].
  • DR-reported, not verified against cached papers (not used as annotation evidence): L535Q TM variant mistargets KASH5 to mitochondria (Bentebbal et al. 2021); p.Arg424Thrfs*20 family with NOA + diminished ovarian reserve/recurrent miscarriage (Hou 2023, PMID:36864840 per UniProt); possible link to recurrent androgenetic hydatidiform mole (Rezaei 2024, PMID:39545410).
  • DR cites Garner et al. with a bioRxiv DOI; the published JCB paper is PMID:36946995 (cached).

id: Q8N6L0
gene_symbol: KASH5
product_type: PROTEIN
status: COMPLETE
taxon:
  id: NCBITaxon:9606
  label: Homo sapiens
description: KASH5 (formerly CCDC155) is a meiosis-specific, tail-anchored KASH protein of the outer nuclear
  membrane, expressed in spermatocytes and oocytes. Its short C-terminal KASH peptide (residues ~542-562,
  divergent from nesprins and carrying a 545-PPP-547 motif) crosses into the perinuclear space and binds
  SUN domain trimers of SUN1 (and SUN2), forming the meiotic LINC complex as SUN-KASH 6:6 assemblies at
  sites where telomeres attach to the nuclear envelope from leptotene to diplotene. Its large cytoplasmic
  region comprises an N-terminal EF-hand pair and a long coiled coil that mediates homodimerization; the
  EF-hands bind the dynein light intermediate chain, making KASH5 a transmembrane dynein activating adaptor
  that assembles dynein-dynactin (with LIS1) into processive complexes. By coupling telomeres through
  SUN1/SUN2 to cytoplasmic dynein and microtubules, KASH5 drives the rapid telomere-led chromosome movements
  and telomere clustering of meiotic prophase I that promote homologue pairing and synapsis. Kash5-null
  mice of both sexes are infertile with prophase I arrest, and biallelic human KASH5 variants cause non-obstructive
  azoospermia (SPGF88) and premature ovarian insufficiency (POF22). In mouse oocytes KASH5 also relocates
  to the meiotic spindle poles and has been implicated in meiotic resumption and spindle formation.

  review:
    summary: KASH5 is concentrated at nuclear-envelope sites where meiotic telomeres attach, from leptotene
      to diplotene.
    action: MARK_AS_OVER_ANNOTATED
    reason: KASH5 co-localizes with telomeres at light-microscopy resolution, but it is a tail-anchored
      outer nuclear membrane protein whose bulk faces the cytoplasm and whose KASH peptide reaches only
      the perinuclear space; it contacts telomeres indirectly via SUN1/SUN2 across the inner nuclear membrane.
      It is therefore not a component of the chromosomal telomeric region. Its location is better captured
      by nuclear outer membrane (GO:0005640) and the meiotic LINC complex term (GO:0034993).

        with telomeres on the opposite outsides of the NE, thus transferring the driving force generated
        by cytoplasmic microtubules to telomeres in the nucleus.

  review:
    summary: High-throughput binary interaction (Rolland et al. 2014 proteome-scale yeast two-hybrid map
      (HI-II-14)) with UniProtKB:A8K660.
    action: REMOVE
    reason: GO:0005515 protein binding is uninformative. Removal does not imply the reported interaction
      is false, but this single systematic screen hit does not indicate what KASH5 does. Most KASH5 high-throughput
      Y2H partners are unrelated transmembrane or ER/Golgi proteins (SNAREs, SLC transporters, TMEMs),
      a pattern typical of the hydrophobic C-terminal tail-anchor of KASH5 rather than a biological role;
      none has been connected to KASH5 function in meiocytes. The biologically established partners (SUN1/SUN2,
      dynein light intermediate chain, dynactin, LIS1) are captured by other terms.

  review:
    summary: High-throughput binary interaction (Luck et al. 2020 HuRI reference binary interactome (yeast
      two-hybrid)) with UniProtKB:Q9UL15.
    action: REMOVE
    reason: GO:0005515 protein binding is uninformative. Removal does not imply the reported interaction
      is false, but this single systematic screen hit does not indicate what KASH5 does. Most KASH5 high-throughput
      Y2H partners are unrelated transmembrane or ER/Golgi proteins (SNAREs, SLC transporters, TMEMs),
      a pattern typical of the hydrophobic C-terminal tail-anchor of KASH5 rather than a biological role;
      none has been connected to KASH5 function in meiocytes. The biologically established partners (SUN1/SUN2,
      dynein light intermediate chain, dynactin, LIS1) are captured by other terms.

    summary: Chromosome localization to nuclear envelope involved in homologous chromosome segregation.
    action: KEEP_AS_NON_CORE
    reason: KASH5 is required for proper NE anchoring of telomeres in human patient spermatocytes (L197P),

core_functions:
- description: 'Transmembrane dynein activating adaptor of the outer nuclear membrane: the N-terminal
    EF-hand pair binds the dynein light intermediate chain and, with the coiled coil, assembles dynein-dynactin-LIS1
    into processive motors that pull on telomere attachment sites to drive meiotic telomere clustering
    and homologue pairing.'

suggested_questions:
- question: Should the oocyte-derived IBA/IEA terms (actin filament organization, spindle localization,
    spindle assembly) remain on the KASH5 PAINT node, given they rest on a single mouse knockdown study
    with indirect phenotypes?
  experts:
  - PAINT curators
  - MGI curators
- question: How is the SUN1-KASH5 6:6 stoichiometry reconciled with the KASH5 homodimer that binds a single
    dynein LIC, and does SUN1 versus SUN2 pairing change force transmission at telomeres?
  experts:
  - LINC complex structural biologists
  - dynein biophysicists
- question: Is the KASH5 EF-hand pair calcium-independent in vivo, and is KASH5 dynein activation regulated
    during prophase I (e.g. by CDK or other kinases)?
  experts:
  - meiosis cell biologists
suggested_experiments:
- description: Knock-in mice carrying the KASH5 EF-hand/LIC-interface mutation that abolishes dynein binding
    in vitro, with live imaging of telomere movement and scoring of pairing/synapsis.
  hypothesis: Dynein activation by KASH5, rather than KASH5 presence at the NE per se, is required for
    telomere clustering and homologue pairing.
  experiment_type: knock-in genetics with live imaging
- description: Oocyte-specific conditional Kash5 deletion after prophase I (e.g. Gdf9- or Zp3-Cre) to
    test spindle assembly and meiotic resumption phenotypes reported by knockdown.
  hypothesis: KASH5 has a genuine post-prophase role at oocyte spindle poles.
  experiment_type: conditional knockout
